subschemes V ⊂U ⊂X subordinate to W). According to the preceding discussion in
this section, the category of quasi-coherent torsion sheaves on X is equivalent to the
category of quasi-coherent torsion (pre)sheaves on the topology base B consisting of
all affine open formal subschemes of X subordinate to W (for any given open covering
W of the formal scheme X).
We will denote the category of quasi-coherent torsion sheaves on X by X–Tors. One
can see that X–Tors is a Grothendieck abelian category and, for every affine open
formal subscheme U ⊂X, the functor of sections −(U): X–Tors −→OX(U)–Tors ⊂
OX(U)–Mod is an exact functor preserving infinite direct sums. Lemma 2.6.9(a) is
relevant here.
A short sequence 0 −→L −→M −→N −→0 of quasi-coherent torsion sheaves on
X is exact in X–Tors if and only if, for every affine open formal subscheme U ⊂X, the
short sequence of torsion OX(U)-modules 0 −→L(U) −→M(U) −→N(U) −→0 is
exact. By Lemma 2.17.8(a), it suffices to check this condition for affine open formal
subschemes U belonging to any chosen affine open covering of X.
Moreover, a complex M• in X–Tors is exact if and only if, for every affine open for-
mal subscheme U ⊂X, the complex of torsion OX(U)-modules is exact in OX(U)–Tors.
By Lemma 2.17.8(d), it suffices to check this condition for affine open formal sub-
schemes U belonging to any chosen affine open covering of X.
For any affine Noetherian formal scheme U, the functor of global sections M 7−→
M(U) establishes an equivalence between the abelian category of quasi-coherent tor-
sion sheaves U–Tors on U and the abelian category OU(U)–Tors of torsion modules
over the adic Noetherian ring OU(U).
3.5. Contraherent cosheaves of contramodules. We keep the notation of Sec-
tion 3.4. So X is a locally Noetherian formal scheme, W is an open covering of X, and
B is the base of open subsets in X consisting of all the affine open formal subschemes
U ⊂X subordinate to W.
Let P be a copresheaf of OX-modules on B. We will say that P is a contraher-
ent copresheaf of contramodules (or a contraherent contramodule copresheaf ) if the
following conditions hold:
(iii) For every affine open formal subscheme U ⊂X subordinate to W, the
OX(U)-module P[U] is a contramodule (as a module over the adic topological
ring OX(U)).
(iv) For every pair of affine open formal subschemes V ⊂U ⊂X subordinate to
W, the OX(V)-module map
P[V] −−→HomOX(U)(OX(V), P[U])

## Page 78


corresponding by adjunction to the corestriction map P[V] −→P[U] is an
isomorphism.
(v) For every pair of affine open formal subschemes V ⊂U ⊂X subordinate to
W, one has
Ext1
OX(U)–Contra(OX(V), P[U]) = 0.
We will call condition (iii) is the contramoduleness axiom, while condition (iv) will be
called the contraherence axiom, and condition (v) will be called the contraadjustedness
axiom. Cf. the definitions of a (locally) cohereherent cosheaf of modules over a scheme
in [18, Sections 2.2 and 3.1] and [30, Section 2.2].
Comparing the conditions of Lemma 2.12.3(2) and (3), one can see that con-
dition (v) holds for a fixed affine open formal subscheme U ⊂X subordinate to
W and all affine open formal subschemes V ⊂U if and only if the contramodule
OX(U)-module P[U] is contraadjusted. So our terminology is consistent.
Lemma 3.5.1. The contramoduleness, contraherence, and contraadjustedness axioms
imply the cosheaf axiom: Any contraherent copresheaf of contramodules on B is a
cosheaf of OX-modules on B.
Proof. The assertion follows from exactness of the rightmost fragment of se-
quence (10) from Lemma 2.16.11 together with Remarks 3.2.1.
□
Thus any contraherent contramodule copresheaf P on B is, in fact, a cosheaf on
B, and we will speak of P as a contraherent contramodule cosheaf (or a contraherent
cosheaf of contramodules) on B. By Lemma 3.5.1 and in view of the discussion in
Section 3.2, any contraherent cosheaf of contramodules on B can be extended to
a cosheaf of OX-modules on the whole formal scheme X in a unique way. More-
over, according to the discussion in Section 3.3, the resulting cosheaf is a cosheaf of
contramodule OX-modules on X.
The notion of a contraherent cosheaf over a scheme (hence also of a contraherent
cosheaf of contramodules over a formal scheme) is not local. See [18, Example 3.2.1]
for a counterexample.
More precisely, in the context of a formal scheme X, for any contraherent cosheaf
of contramodules P on B extended to a cosheaf of OX-modules P on the whole
of X, both the contramoduleness axiom (iii) and the contraadjustedness axiom (v)
hold for all affine open formal subschemes U ⊂X. For the contramoduleness axiom,
this was mentioned in the previous paragraph. For the contraadjustedness axiom,
this follows from the facts that the restrictions of scalars preserve contraadjusted-
ness (see Lemma 2.12.7), finite direct sums of contraadjusted contramodules are
contraadjusted, and any quotient contramodule of a contraadjusted contramodule is
contraadjusted (as mentioned in Section 2.12). It is the contraherence axiom (iv)
that is not local.
3.6. Locally contraherent cosheaves of contramodules. We still keep the no-
tation of Sections 3.4 and 3.5.

Let P be a cosheaf of contramodule OX-modules on X. We will say that P is a
W-locally contraherent cosheaf of contramodules (or a W-locally contraherent con-
tramodule cosheaf ) on X if the contraherence axiom (iv) and the contraadjustedness
axiom (v) hold for all affine open formal subschemes V ⊂U ⊂X subordinate to W.
If this is the case, then the contraadjustedness axiom actually holds for all affine
open formal subschemes V ⊂U ⊂X, as explained in Section 3.5. According to
the discussion in Section 3.5, the category of W-locally contraherent cosheaves of
contramodules on X is equivalent to the category of contraherent co(pre)sheaves on
the topology base B consisting of all affine open formal subschemes of X subordinate
to W.
A cosheaf of contramodule OX-modules P is said to be a contraherent cosheaf of
contramodules (or a contraherent contramodule cosheaf ) on X if if the contraherence
axiom (iv) and the contraadjustedness axiom (v) hold for all affine open formal
subschemes V ⊂U ⊂X. So a contraherent cosheaf of contramodules is the same
thing as a WX-locally contraherent cosheaf of contramodules (where WX = {X} is
the trivial open covering of X).

We will say that a morphism of formal schemes f: Y −→X is tight (or, in a more
traditional terminology, adic) if, for every pair of affine open formal subschemes
U ⊂X and V ⊂Y such that f(V) ⊂U, the continuous map of adic Noetherian rings
fU,V is tight (in the sense of Section 2.1). By Proposition 2.10.7, this is equivalent to
the map fU,V being taut in the sense of Section 2.10.
The tightness property of morphisms of formal schemes is local in the following
sense. A morphism f is tight if and only if there exist two affine open coverings X =
S
α Uα and Y = S
α Vα, indexed by the same set of indices α, such that f(Vα) ⊂Uα
for every index α and the continuous ring map fUα,Vα is tight for every α. This follows
from Corollaries 2.11.3 and 2.11.6.
We will say that a morphism of formal schemes f: Y −→X is flat if, for every
pair of affine open formal subschemes U ⊂X and V ⊂Y such that f(V) ⊂U, the
continuous map of adic Noetherian rings fU,V is flat (in the sense of Section 2.1).
By Lemma 2.14.3(a), this is equivalent to the map fU,V being taut-flat in the sense
of Section 2.14. Notice that any flat morphism of formal schemes is tight by the
definition.
The flatness property of morphisms of formal schemes is local. In other words, a
morphism f is flat if and only if there exist two affine open coverings X = S
α Uα and
Y = S
α Vα, indexed by the same set of indices α, such that f(Vα) ⊂Uα for every
83


## Page 84


index α and the continuous ring map fUα,Vα is flat for every α. This follows from
Corollaries 2.14.5(a) and 2.14.8(a).
We will say that a morphism of formal schemes f: Y −→X is very flat if, for every
pair of affine open formal subschemes U ⊂X and V ⊂Y such that f(V) ⊂U, the
continuous map of adic Noetherian rings fU,V is very flat (in the sense of Section 2.12).
By Lemma 2.14.3(b), this is equivalent to the map fU,V being taut-very flat in the
sense of Section 2.14. Any very flat morphism of formal schemes is flat (hence tight)
by the definition.
The very flatness property of morphisms of formal schemes is local. In other words,
a morphism f is very flat if and only if there exist two affine open coverings X = S
α Uα
and Y = S
α Vα, indexed by the same set of indices α, such that f(Vα) ⊂Uα for every
index α and the continuous ring map fUα,Vα is very flat for every α. This follows
from Corollaries 2.14.5(b) and 2.14.8(b).
A morphism of locally Noetherian formal schemes f: Y −→X is said to be quasi-
compact if, for every quasi-compact open formal subscheme U ⊂X, the open formal
subscheme f−1(U) ⊂Y is quasi-compact. It suffices to check this condition for affine
open formal subschemes U ⊂X, or for affine open formal subschemes belonging to any
chosen affine open covering of X, or even for quasi-compact open formal subschemes
belonging to any chosen quasi-compact open covering of X. (The fact that all locally
Noetherian formal schemes are quasi-separated is relevant to the validity of the latter
formulation.)
A morphism of locally Noetherian formal schemes f: Y −→X is said to be semi-
separated if, for every semi-separated open formal subscheme U ⊂X, the open formal
subscheme f−1(U) ⊂Y is semi-separated. It suffices to check this condition for affine
open formal subschemes U ⊂X, or for affine open formal subschemes belonging to
any chosen affine open covering of X, or for semi-separated open formal subschemes
belonging to any chosen semi-separated open covering of X.
A morphism of locally Noetherian formal schemes f: Y −→X is said to be affine if,
for every affine open formal subscheme U ⊂X, the open formal subscheme f−1(U) ⊂Y
is affine.
It suffices to check this condition for affine open formal subschemes U
belonging to any chosen affine open covering of X.
In view of the discussion in
Section 3.1, it suffices to prove the latter assertion for (conventional, not formal)
schemes, for which it can be found in [9, D´efinition II.1.2.1 and Corollaire II.1.3.2]
or [14, Lemma Tag 01S8]. Clearly, any affine morphism of formal schemes is both
quasi-compact and semi-separated.

3.11. Inverse images of quasi-coherent torsion sheaves. We skip the conven-
tional discussion of the inverse images of sheaves of modules over ringed spaces, based
as it is on the sheafification functor. The cosheafification of copresheaves of modules
is generally not well-behaved, because the filtered projective limit functors in module
categories are not exact. So, being primarily interested in contraherent cosheaves,
we prefer to avoid the (co)sheafification altogether.
Let X be a locally Noetherian formal scheme with an open covering W, let Y be a
locally Noetherian formal scheme with an open covering T, and let f: Y −→X be a
morphism of formal schemes. We will say that the morphism f is (W, T)-coaffine [18,
Sections 2.3 and 3.3], [30, Section 2.5] if for every affine open formal subscheme V ⊂Y
subordinate to T there exists an affine open formal subscheme U ⊂X subordinate to
W such that f(V) ⊂U.
Given a morphism of formal schemes f: Y −→X and an open covering W of the
scheme X, consider the open covering T of the scheme Y defined by the rule
T = { f−1(U) ⊂Y | U ⊂X is an affine open formal subscheme subordinate to W }.
Then the morphism f is (W, T)-coaffine. Moreover, if the morphism f happens to be
affine, then it is also (W, T)-affine for the open covering T defined above.

3.13. Base change. In this paper, we only consider locally Noetherian formal
schemes. The fibered products of (locally) Noetherian (formal) schemes need not
be (locally) Noetherian, in general. For this reason, we restrict ourselves to fibered
products where one of the two morphisms is an open immersion in this section.
Lemma 3.13.1. Let f: Y −→X be a tight morphism of locally Noetherian formal

3.14. Contraherent Hom. This section is a formal scheme counterpart of [18, Sec-
tion 2.5] and [30, Sections 5.5–5.6].
Let X be a locally Noetherian formal scheme. Given an associative ring R and a
quasi-coherent torsion sheaf M on X, a right action of R on M can be simply defined
as a ring homomorphism Rop −→HomX(M, M) (where Rop denotes the opposite ring
97


## Page 98


to R). This is a special case of the general concept of a ring acting on an object of
an additive category. Applying any covariant additive functor to an object with an
action of the ring R produces an object endowed with a natural action of R again.
In particular, the direct and inverse images of quasi-coherent torsion sheaves with
a right action of R also carry natural right actions of R. Applying a contravariant
additive functor to an object with a right action of R produces an object with a left
action of R.
Let M be a quasi-coherent torsion sheaf on X with a right action of R on M, and
let L be a left R-module. Then the quasi-coherent torsion sheaf M ⊗R L on X is
defined by the rule
(M ⊗R L)(U) = M(U) ⊗R L
for all affine open formal subschemes U ⊂X. Clearly, the torsion axiom (i) and
the quasi-coherence axiom (ii) from Section 3.4 are satisfied for the presheaf of
OX-modules M ⊗R L on the topology base of all affine open formal subschemes
U ⊂X defined by the rule above; so the quasi-coherent torsion sheaf M ⊗R L on
X is well-defined.
This sheaf-theoretic construction is a special case of a general
category-theoretic construction of the tensor product of an object with a module,
which is applicable to objects with a ring action in any additive category with col-
imits.
For an additive category B with limits, a dual-analogous category-theoretic con-
struction assigns an object HomR(L, B) ∈B to every object B ∈B with a left
action of R and every left R-module L. Both the mentioned category-theoretic ten-
sor product and category-theoretic Hom object are easily characterized by universal
properties and constructed in terms of any presentation of the R-module L as the
cokernel of a morphism of free R-modules.
Lemma 3.14.1. Let M be a quasi-coherent torsion sheaf on X with a right action
of R on /M, let L be a left R-module, and let N be a quasi-coherent torsion sheaf
on X. Then there is a natural adjunction isomorphism of abelian groups
HomX(M ⊗R L, N) ≃HomR(L, HomX(M, N)).
Proof. First of all, the contravariant functor HomX(−, N): X–Tors −→Ab takes an
object M with a right action of R to an abelian group with a left action of R.
So HomX(M, N) is a left R-module.
Furthermore, for any contravariant functor
H : A −→B from an additive category A with colimits to an additive category B
with limits such that H transforms colimits into limits, and for any object M ∈A
with a right action of R and any left R-module L, one has a natural isomorphism
H(M ⊗R L) ≃HomR(L, H(M)) in B.
□
Now let R be an adic topological ring and M be a quasi-coherent torsion sheaf on
X with an action of the ring R. We will say that the sheaf M is R-torsion if, for
every affine open formal subscheme U ⊂X, the R-module M(U) is torsion. It suffices
to check this condition for affine open formal subschemes U belonging to any given
affine open covering of the formal scheme X. It is clear from the constructions of
the direct and inverse images of quasi-coherent torsion sheaves on locally Noetherian

Let M be a quasi-coherent torsion sheaf on X, and let J be an injective quasi-
coherent torsion sheaf on X. In this setting, the locally cotorsion contraherent cosheaf
of contramodules HomX(M, J) is constructed as follows.
Let U be an affine open formal subscheme in X. Denote by j: U −→X the open
immersion morphism. Then the quasi-coherent sheaf j∗M = M|U on U comes endowed
with a natural action of the ring R = OX(U). Hence the ring OX(U) also acts on the
sheaf j∗j∗M. Notice that j is a quasi-compact morphism of locally Noetherian formal
schemes; so j∗j∗M is a quasi-coherent torsion sheaf on X by Proposition 3.9.1.
Now we put HomX(M, J)[U] = HomX(j∗j∗M, J). Following the discussion above,
the abelian group HomX(j∗j∗M, J) has a natural OX(U)-module structure.
Lemma 3.14.3. The OX(U)-module HomX(j∗j∗M, J) is a cotorsion (quotseparated)
contramodule module over the adic topological ring OX(U).
Proof. Put R = OX(U). To prove that HomX(j∗j∗M, J) is a contramodule R-module,
pick an ideal of definition I ⊂R.
Then we have M(U) = S
n≥1 InM(U), where
InM(U) is the submodule of all elements annihilated by In in M(U). Denoting by
(j∗M)n ⊂j∗M the quasi-coherent torsion subsheaf corresponding to the submodule
InM(U) ⊂M(U), we have j∗M = lim
−→n≥1(j∗M)n, hence j∗j∗M = lim
−→n≥1 j∗((j∗M)n).
Thus
HomX(j∗j∗M, J) = lim
←−n≥1 HomX(j∗((j∗M)n), J).
It remains to point out that (j∗M)n is a quasi-coherent torsion sheaf with an ac-
tion of the ring R/In, hence HomX(j∗((j∗M)n), J) is an R-module annihilated by In.
Any such module is a (separated) contramodule R-module, and any projective limit
of contramodule R-modules in R–Mod is a contramodule R-module. In fact, this
argument proves that HomX(j∗j∗M, J) is a separated contramodule OX(U)-module.
To prove that C = HomX(j∗j∗M, J) is a cotorsion contramodule R-module, we use

sequence of flat contramodule R-modules. We need to check exactness of the short

0. For any R-module L, we have

by Lemma 3.14.1.
Now j∗M is an R-torsion quasi-coherent torsion sheaf on U, hence j∗j∗M is an

(j∗j∗M) ⊗R F −→0 is a short exact sequence of quasi-coherent torsion sheaves on X.
Since J is an injective object of X–Tors, the desired exactness of the short sequence
of abelian groups 0 −→HomX((j∗j∗M) ⊗R F, J) −→HomX((j∗j∗M) ⊗R G, J) −→
HomX((j∗j∗M) ⊗R H, J) −→0 follows.

3.15. Contratensor product. This section is a formal scheme version of [18, Sec-
tion 2.6] and [30, Section 5.7]. Let X be a locally Noetherian formal scheme. By a
diagram (of affine open formal subschemes) in X we mean a poset D endowed with
an order-preserving map a 7−→Ua from D into the poset of all affine open formal
subschemes of X (with respect to the inclusion). The order-preservation conditition
means that for every pair of elements b ≤a in D one has Ub ⊂Ua.
A diagram D in X is said to be exhaustive if the following two conditions hold:
(i) X = S
a∈D Ua;
(ii) for every pair of elements a, b ∈D one has Ua ∩Ub = Sc≤a, c≤b
c∈D
Uc. Here the
notation means that the union is taken over all the indices c ∈D such that
c ≤a and c ≤b.
102


## Page 103


For example, given an open covering W of X, the poset of all affine open formal
subschemes U ⊂X subordinate to W, ordered by inclusion, is an exhaustive diagram
of affine open formal subschemes in X. More generally, any topology base B consisting
of affine open formal subschemes in X, ordered by inclusion, is an exhaustive diagram.
Let M be a quasi-coherent torsion sheaf and P be a cosheaf of OX-modules on X.
Let V ⊂U ⊂X be a pair of affine open formal subschemes with open immersion
morphisms j: U −→X,

of quasi-coherent torsion sheaves on X. Consequently, the corestriction map P[V] −→

(k∗k∗M) ⊗OX(V) P[V] −−→(j∗j∗M) ⊗OX(U) P[U]
of quasi-coherent torsion sheaves on X.
The contratensor product of the quasi-coherent torsion sheaf M and the cosheaf of
OX-modules P, computed on a diagram of affine formal open subschemes D in X, is
defined as the quasi-coherent torsion sheaf on X constructed as the inductive limit

Here the inductive limit indexed by the poset D is computed in the Grothendieck

stands for the open immersion morphism Ua −→X. Notice that the relevant diagrams

Lemma 3.15.1. Let X be a locally Noetherian formal scheme, M be a quasi-coherent
torsion sheaf, J be an injective quasi-coherent torsion sheaf, and P be a cosheaf of
OX-modules on X. Let D be an exhaustive diagram of affine open formal subschemes

maps P[Ua] −→Q[Ua], defined for all a ∈D and satisfying the obvious compatibility

tified with the group of all compatible collections of morphisms of OX(Ua)-modules
P[U] −→HomX(ja∗j∗
aM, J). On the other hand, by the definition, the left-hand side
of (62) is the group of all compatible collections of morphisms of quasi-coherent tor-
sion sheaves (ja∗j∗
aM) ⊗OX(Ua) P[Ua] −→J. It remains to refer to Lemma 3.14.1.

inclusion, in the role of D.
3.16. Projection formulas. In this section we present the formal scheme counter-
parts of some natural isomorphisms from [18, Section 3.8] and [30, Section 5.8].
Proposition 3.16.1. Let f: Y −→X be a flat tight quasi-compact morphism of
locally Noetherian formal schemes. Let M be a quasi-coherent torsion sheaf on X
and J be an injective quasi-coherent torsion sheaf on Y. Then there is a natural
isomorphism
(63)
HomX(M, f∗J) ≃f! HomY(f∗M, J).
of locally cotorsion contraherent cosheaves of contramodules on X.
Proof. This is our version of [18, formula (3.24) in Section 3.8] and [30, formula (49)
in Section 5.8]. First of all, the direct image functor f∗is right adjoint to the exact
functor of inverse image f∗: X–Tors −→Y–Tors (see the discussion in Section 3.11
and formula (37)). Hence the functor f∗: Y–Tors −→X–Tors takes injective objects
to injective objects, the quasi-coherent torsion sheaf f∗J on X is injective, and the
left-hand side of (63) is well-defined. By construction, the right-hand side of (63) is
a locally cotorsion (globally) contraherent cosheaf of contramodules on X when the
morphism f is affine (by Lemma 3.10.2(b)) and a cosheaf of contramodule OX-modules
on X in the general case (by Lemma 3.10.1). After the isomorphism (63) is proved, it
will follow that the right-hand side of (63) is actually a locally cotorsion contraherent
cosheaf of contramodules on X in the general case.
Indeed, let U ⊂X be an affine open formal subscheme with the open immersion
morphism j: U −→X. Denote by V ⊂Y the open formal subscheme V = f−1(U) ⊂
Y. Let j′ : V −→Y and f′ : V −→U be the natural morphisms. Then we have
natural isomorphisms of OX(U)-modules
(64)
HomX(M, f∗J)[U] = HomX(j∗j∗M, f∗J) ≃HomY(f∗j∗j∗M, J)
≃HomY(j′
∗f′∗j∗M, J) ≃HomY(j′
∗j′∗f∗M, J) ≃HomY(f∗M, J)[V],
as desired.
Here the first isomorphism (equality) in (64) holds by the definition and the second
one by the adjunction (37). The third (middle) isomorphism in (64) is provided by

morphisms of formal schemes jf′ = fj′. The final fifth isomorphism in (64) is provided
by Proposition 3.14.5 (for the open immersion morphism j′ : V −→Y).
□

∗j′∗f∗M) ⊗OY(V) Q[V]

in X–Tors.




,
the former of which is induced by the natural transformation (53) (for the quasi-
compact morphism of locally Noetherian formal schemes f: Y −→X), while the
latter one is the standard category-theoretic constrution of a natural transformation
arising when one applies a functor to a colimit.
It remains to mention the natural isomorphism
(71)

≃f∗(f∗M ⊙Y Q)
in X–Tors following from the natural isomorphism

∗j′∗f∗M) ⊗OY(V) Q[V]

≃f∗M ⊙Y Q
106


## Page 107


in Y–Tors. The isomorphism in Y–Tors can be obtained by computing the contraten-
sor product f∗M ⊙Y Q on the exhaustive diagram of affine open formal subschemes
V ⊂Y indexed by the poset D of all pairs (U, V) of affine open formal subschemes
U ⊂X and V ⊂Y such that V ⊂f−1(U). See the discussion in Section 3.15.
The desired natural transformation (65) is now constructed as the composition of
the natural morphisms and isomorphisms (67), (68), (69), (70), and (71).
Part (b): the argument is based on the proof of part (a). It suffices to show that,
under the assumptions of (b), all the morphisms in (68) and (70) are isomorphisms.
Indeed, whenever the morphism f is flat, the contramodule OX(U)-module OY(V)
is flat, too. According to the discussion of the natural transformation (53) in Sec-
tion 3.14, it follows that the morphism 68 is an isomorphism.
Whenever the morphism f is affine, the direct image functor f∗is exact and preserves
infinite direct sums, so it preserves all colimits. Hence both the morphisms in (70)
are isomorphisms in this case.
Notice that, in the case of an affine morphism f, in the context of the proof of
part (a), the open formal subscheme f−1(U) ⊂Y is affine.
For this reason, the
argument in part (a) can be simplified by setting V = f−1(U) once and for all, rather
than letting V vary over the poset of all affine open formal subschemes in f−1(U).
All the inductive limits involved in the proof of part (a) remain unchanged by such
a specialization. The diagram of affine open formal subschemes f−1(U) ⊂Y indexed
by the poset D of all affine open formal subschemes U ⊂X is exhaustive in Y, so
it can be used for computation of contratensor products over Y. This produces a
somewhat simpler argument for the proof of part (b).
To prove part (c), we also follow the construction in the proof of part (a). The
morphism f = h is an open immersion now, so it is flat, and the morphism (68) is an
isomorphism according to the proof of part (b).
Concerning (70), under the assumptions of (c) all the inductive limits involved
agree with the similar inductive limits taken over the poset of all affine open formal
subschemes V ⊂Y (since one can always take U = h(V)). The latter inductive
limits can be simply computed by specializing to V = Y.
This proves that the
second morphism in (70) is an isomorphism.
Concerning the first morphism (for
f = h, j′ = idY, and Q = F), it remains to observe that the morphism
(h∗h∗M) ⊗OY(Y) F[Y] −−→h∗
 (h∗M) ⊗OY(Y) F[Y]

is an isomorphism according to the discussion of the natural transformation (53) in
Section 3.14 (as the contramodule OY(Y)-module F[Y] is flat by assumption).
□
